Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive report.

## HTTP Client Creation and Usage in Moltis Codebase

Based on my thorough exploration, here's a complete analysis of HTTP client creation patterns throughout the moltis codebase:

### 1. **Shared HTTP Client Pattern (Recommended)**

The codebase uses a centralized shared HTTP client to avoid per-request overhead. There are two main shared client implementations:

**Tools Crate** (`/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/tools/src/lib.rs`):
```rust
// Lines 19-49
static SHARED_CLIENT: std::sync::OnceLock<reqwest::Client> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();

/// Initialize the shared HTTP client with optional proxy.
pub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {
    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(build_http_client(proxy_url));
}

/// Shared HTTP client for tools that don't need custom configuration.
pub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {
    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)
}

/// Build a `reqwest::Client` with optional proxy configuration.
pub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {
    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();
    if let Some(url) = proxy_url
        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)
    {
        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string("localhost,127.0.0.1,::1"));
        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);
    }
    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())
}
```

**Providers Crate** (`/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/providers/src/lib.rs`):
```rust
// Lines 46-54
/// Shared HTTP client for LLM providers.
///
/// All providers that don't need custom redirect/proxy settings should
/// reuse this client to share connection pools, DNS cache, and TLS sessions.
pub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {
    static CLIENT: std::sync::LazyLock<reqwest::Client> =
        std::sync::LazyLock::new(reqwest::Client::new);
    &CLIENT
}
```

### 2. **Initialization in Gateway**

Gateway startup initializes the shared HTTP client with proxy URL when trusted-network policy is enabled (`/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/gateway/src/server.rs`, lines 2120-2176):

```rust
if sandbox_config.network == moltis_network_filter::NetworkPolicy::Trusted {
    // ... proxy setup ...
    let url = format!(
        "http://127.0.0.1:{}",
        moltis_network_filter::DEFAULT_PROXY_PORT
    );
    moltis_tools::init_shared_http_client(Some(&url));
}
```

### 3. **Per-Request HTTP Client Creation**

The following places create new `reqwest::Client::new()` instances (not ideal but necessary for specific use cases):

**OAuth & Device Flow** (`crates/oauth/src/flow.rs`, `device_flow.rs`, `discovery.rs`):
- Line 36 in `flow.rs`: OAuth flow creates a new client per flow instance
- Multiple instances in `device_flow.rs` (lines 244, 272, 290, 308, 335, 384, 405, 425)
- Multiple instances in `discovery.rs` for OIDC discovery

**Tools with Custom Configuration** (`crates/tools/src/web_fetch.rs`, lines 162-171):
```rust
let mut client_builder = reqwest::Client::builder()
    .timeout(self.timeout)
    .redirect(reqwest::redirect::Policy::none());
if let Some(ref url) = self.proxy_url
    && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)
{
    let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string("localhost,127.0.0.1,::1"));
    client_builder = client_builder.proxy(proxy);
}
let client = client_builder.build()?;
```

**Slack Channel** (`crates/slack/src/outbound.rs`, line 114):
```rust
let http = reqwest::Client::new();
```

**MS Teams Channel** (`crates/msteams/src/plugin.rs`, line 393):
```rust
http: reqwest::Client::new(),
```

**Voice/TTS Providers** (ElevenLabs, Google TTS, Coqui, OpenAI TTS, etc.):
- Each voice provider creates `Client::new()` in their `new()` or `with_alias()` methods

**Telegram** (`crates/telegram/src/bot.rs`, lines 36-39):
```rust
let client = teloxide::net::default_reqwest_settings()
    .timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(45))
    .build()
    .map_err(|source| crate::Error::external("build telegram client", source))?;
let bot = Bot::with_client(config.token.expose_secret(), client);
```

**Local LLM Models** (`crates/providers/src/local_llm/models.rs`, lines 726, 763):
- Creates clients for model discovery endpoints

**Other instances**:
- MCP auth (`crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`, lines 118, 140)
- CLI commands (`crates/cli/src/node_commands.rs`, `auth_commands.rs`)
- Skills installer (`crates/skills/src/install.rs`)
- Memory embeddings (`crates/memory/src/embeddings_batch.rs`, `embeddings_openai.rs`)

### 4. **Workspace Dependencies**

Root `Cargo.toml` defines `reqwest` with features:
```toml
reqwest = { features = ["json", "multipart", "stream"], version = "0.12" }
```

Most crates use `{ workspace = true }`, with exceptions:
- `tools` crate: `{ features = ["blocking"], workspace = true }`
- `voice` crate: `{ features = ["multipart"], workspace = true }`

### 5. **Network Configuration Schema**

`crates/config/src/schema.rs` (lines 1972-1978):
```rust
pub no_network: bool,
/// Network policy: "blocked" (no network), "trusted" (proxy-filtered), "bypass" (unrestricted, no audit).
#[serde(default)]
pub network: String,
/// Domains allowed through the proxy in `trusted` mode.
#[serde(default)]
pub trusted_domains: Vec<String>,
```

### 6. **Proxy Support Implementation**

**Network Filter Proxy** (`crates/network-filter/src/proxy.rs`):
- HTTP CONNECT proxy server for domain filtering
- Listens on `127.0.0.1:DEFAULT_PROXY_PORT` (typically 19790)
- Handles both CONNECT (HTTPS) and plain HTTP forward requests
- Validates against allowed private IP ranges, rejects public IPs

**Web Fetch Tool** has proxy support:
- `WebFetchTool::with_proxy()` method allows setting proxy URL
- Creates `ClientBuilder` with proxy when URL is set
- Implements `no_proxy` for localhost/127.0.0.1/::1

### 7. **Key Patterns**

| Pattern | Usage | Location |
|---------|-------|----------|
| **Shared Global** | Tools, web_fetch, firecrawl fallback | `moltis_tools::shared_http_client()` |
| **Shared Global** | LLM providers | `moltis_providers::shared_http_client()` |
| **Per-Request** | OAuth flows, discovery | New `Client::new()` each call |
| **Per-Instance** | Channels (Teams, Slack, Telegram) | Stored in account state |
| **Custom Config** | Web fetch with timeout/proxy | `ClientBuilder::new()` |
| **Library Wrapper** | Slack uses `slack-morphism`, Telegram uses `teloxide` | `SlackClient::new()`, `Bot::with_client()` |

**HTTP Client Creation Points:**

Good, I have a thorough picture of the codebase. Let me summarize the issue and present a plan.

## Issue #548: Application/Channel-level Proxy Support

**Problem:** Users behind corporate firewalls or in restricted networks need to route moltis traffic through an HTTP/SOCKS5 proxy. Currently there's no user-facing proxy config — the only proxy support is the internal sandbox network filter (for domain allowlisting in trusted mode).

**Key findings from codebase exploration:**

1. **Two shared HTTP clients exist, neither supports user proxy:**
   - `moltis_tools::shared_http_client()` — has `init_shared_http_client(proxy_url)` but it's only called with the internal sandbox proxy URL, not a user-configured upstream proxy
   - `moltis_providers::shared_http_client()` — uses plain `reqwest::Client::new()`, no proxy at all

2. **~30+ places create `reqwest::Client::new()` independently** — channels (Telegram, Slack, Teams, Discord, WhatsApp), voice providers, OAuth flows, memory embeddings, MCP auth, etc.

3. **Telegram specifically** uses `teloxide::net::default_reqwest_settings()` which supports proxy via `HTTPS_PROXY` env var, but moltis doesn't set or propagate this.

4. **`reqwest` already supports** `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` env vars natively when `system_proxy` feature is enabled (it is by default).

## Proposed Plan

### Approach: Application-level `upstream_proxy` config field

Add a top-level `upstream_proxy` field to `moltis.toml` that configures a proxy for **all** outbound HTTP traffic (providers, channels, tools, OAuth, etc.).

**Phase 1 — Config & shared client plumbing:**

1. **`crates/config/src/schema.rs`** — Add `upstream_proxy: Option<String>` to the top-level `MoltisConfig` (supports `http://`, `https://`, `socks5://`, `socks5h://`)
2. **`crates/config/src/validate.rs`** — Add validation in `build_schema_map()` and `check_semantic_warnings()` for the new field (warn on malformed URLs)
3. **`crates/config/src/template.rs`** — Document the field in the config template
4. **`crates/tools/src/lib.rs`** — Already has `build_http_client(proxy_url)`. Refactor `init_shared_http_client` to accept the upstream proxy separately from the sandbox proxy, composing both when needed
5. **`crates/providers/src/lib.rs`** — Replace `LazyLock<Client>` with `OnceLock<Client>` + `init_shared_http_client(proxy_url)` mirroring the tools pattern

**Phase 2 — Gateway wiring:**

6. **`crates/gateway/src/server.rs`** — At startup, read `config.upstream_proxy` and pass it to both `moltis_tools::init_shared_http_client()` and `moltis_providers::init_shared_http_client()`. When sandbox trusted mode is also active, chain the upstream proxy through the sandbox proxy.

**Phase 3 — Channel & peripheral client propagation:**

7. **Telegram** (`crates/telegram/src/bot.rs`) — Pass proxy to `reqwest::ClientBuilder` before `Bot::with_client()`
8. **Slack** (`crates/slack/src/outbound.rs`) — Use shared/proxied client instead of `Client::new()`
9. **MS Teams** (`crates/msteams/src/plugin.rs`) — Same
10. **OAuth flows** (`crates/oauth/src/`) — Accept optional proxy URL, build client with it
11. **Voice providers** — Accept optional proxy URL in constructors
12. **Memory embeddings** — Use shared client or accept proxy

**Phase 4 — Environment variable fallback:**

13. `reqwest` already honors `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` by default. The config field takes precedence; if unset, reqwest's built-in env var support kicks in. Document this behavior.

### What this does NOT do (intentionally):
- **Per-channel proxy** — deferred to a follow-up. The application-level proxy covers the 90% case. Per-channel can layer on top later.
- **Proxy auth** — `reqwest::Proxy::all(url)` already supports `http://user:pass@host:port` URLs, so this works out of the box.

### Scope estimate:
- Config schema + validation + template: 3 files
- Shared client refactor: 2 files
- Gateway wiring: 1 file  
- Channel/peripheral propagation: ~8-10 files
- Tests: unit tests for client builder, integration test for proxy config parsing

Want me to proceed with this plan, or would you prefer to adjust scope (e.g., start with just Phase 1+2 and defer channel propagation)?

    69→    type Err = TimezoneParseError;
    70→
    71→    fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
    72→        s.parse::<chrono_tz::Tz>()
    73→            .map(Self)
    74→            .map_err(|_| TimezoneParseError {
    75→                value: s.to_string(),
    76→            })
    77→    }
    78→}
    79→
    80→impl From<chrono_tz::Tz> for Timezone {
    81→    fn from(tz: chrono_tz::Tz) -> Self {
    82→        Self(tz)
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→impl Serialize for Timezone {
    87→    fn serialize<S: serde::Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {
    88→        serializer.serialize_str(self.0.name())
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Timezone {
    93→    fn deserialize<D: serde::Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {
    94→        let s = String::deserialize(deserializer)?;
    95→        s.parse::<Self>().map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
    96→    }
    97→}
    98→
    99→/// Geographic coordinates (WGS 84).
   100→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]

    18→
    19→static SHARED_CLIENT: std::sync::OnceLock<reqwest::Client> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
    20→
    21→/// Initialize the shared HTTP client with optional proxy.
    22→/// Call once at gateway startup; subsequent calls are no-ops.
    23→pub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {
    24→    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(build_http_client(proxy_url));
    25→}
    26→
    27→/// Shared HTTP client for tools that don't need custom configuration.
    28→///
    29→/// Reusing a single `reqwest::Client` avoids per-request connection pool,
    30→/// DNS resolver, and TLS session cache overhead — significant on
    31→/// memory-constrained devices.
    32→///
    33→/// Falls back to a plain client if [`init_shared_http_client`] was never
    34→/// called (e.g. in tests).
    35→pub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {
    36→    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)
    37→}
    38→
    39→/// Build a `reqwest::Client` with optional proxy configuration.
    40→pub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {
    41→    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();
    42→    if let Some(url) = proxy_url
    43→        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)
    44→    {
    45→        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string("localhost,127.0.0.1,::1"));
    46→        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);
    47→    }
    48→    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())
    49→}

     1→//! Configuration validation engine.
     2→//!
     3→//! Validates TOML configuration files against the known schema, detects
     4→//! unknown/misspelled fields, and reports security warnings.
     5→
     6→use std::{collections::HashMap, path::Path};
     7→
     8→use crate::schema::MoltisConfig;
     9→
    10→/// Severity level for a diagnostic.
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    12→pub enum Severity {
    13→    Error,
    14→    Warning,
    15→    Info,
    16→}
    17→
    18→impl std::fmt::Display for Severity {
    19→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    20→        match self {
    21→            Self::Error => write!(f, "error"),
    22→            Self::Warning => write!(f, "warning"),
    23→            Self::Info => write!(f, "info"),
    24→        }
    25→    }
    26→}
    27→
    28→/// A single validation diagnostic.
    29→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    30→pub struct Diagnostic {
    31→    pub severity: Severity,
    32→    /// Category: "syntax", "unknown-field", "deprecated-field", "unknown-provider", "type-error",
    33→    /// "security", "file-ref"
    34→    pub category: &'static str,
    35→    /// Dotted path, e.g. "server.bnd"
    36→    pub path: String,
    37→    pub message: String,
    38→}
    39→
    40→/// Result of validating a configuration file.
    41→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    42→pub struct ValidationResult {
    43→    pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    44→    pub config_path: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
    45→}
    46→
    47→impl ValidationResult {
    48→    /// Returns `true` if any diagnostic is an error.
    49→    #[must_use]
    50→    pub fn has_errors(&self) -> bool {
    51→        self.diagnostics
    52→            .iter()
    53→            .any(|d| d.severity == Severity::Error)
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    /// Count diagnostics by severity.
    57→    #[must_use]
    58→    pub fn count(&self, severity: Severity) -> usize {
    59→        self.diagnostics
    60→            .iter()
    61→            .filter(|d| d.severity == severity)
    62→            .count()
    63→    }
    64→}
    65→
    66→// ── Schema tree for unknown-field detection ─────────────────────────────────
    67→
    68→/// Represents the expected shape of the configuration schema.
    69→enum KnownKeys {
    70→    /// A struct with fixed field names.
    71→    Struct(HashMap<&'static str, KnownKeys>),
    72→    /// A map with dynamic keys (providers, mcp.servers, etc.) whose values
    73→    /// have a known shape.
    74→    Map(Box<KnownKeys>),
    75→    /// A map with dynamic keys plus explicit static keys.
    76→    MapWithFields {
    77→        value: Box<KnownKeys>,
    78→        fields: HashMap<&'static str, KnownKeys>,
    79→    },
    80→    /// An array of typed items.
    81→    Array(Box<KnownKeys>),
    82→    /// Scalar value — stop recursion.
    83→    Leaf,
    84→}
    85→
    86→/// Known LLM provider names for hint diagnostics.
    87→const KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
    88→    "anthropic",
    89→    "openai",
    90→    "gemini",
    91→    "groq",
    92→    "xai",
    93→    "deepseek",
    94→    "fireworks",
    95→    "mistral",
    96→    "openrouter",
    97→    "cerebras",
    98→    "minimax",
    99→    "moonshot",
   100→    "venice",
```

     1→//! Default configuration template with all options documented.
     2→//!
     3→//! This template is used when creating a new config file. It includes all
     4→//! available options with descriptions, allowing users to see everything
     5→//! that can be configured even if they don't change the defaults.
     6→
     7→/// Generate the default config template with a specific port.
     8→pub fn default_config_template(port: u16) -> String {
     9→    format!(
    10→        r##"# Moltis Configuration
    11→# ====================
    12→# This file contains all available configuration options.
    13→# Uncomment and modify settings as needed.
    14→# Changes require a restart to take effect.
    15→#
    16→# Environment variable substitution is supported: ${{ENV_VAR}}
    17→# Example: api_key = "${{ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}}"
    18→
    19→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    20→# SERVER
    21→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    22→
    23→[server]
    24→bind = "127.0.0.1"                # Address to bind to ("0.0.0.0" for all interfaces)
    25→port = {port}                           # Port number (auto-generated for this installation)
    26→http_request_logs = false              # Enable verbose Axum HTTP request/response logs (debugging)
    27→ws_request_logs = false                # Enable WebSocket RPC request/response logs (debugging)
    28→update_releases_url = "https://www.moltis.org/releases.json"    # Releases manifest URL for update checks (override to use a custom URL)
    29→
    30→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    31→# AUTHENTICATION
    32→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    33→
    34→[auth]
    35→disabled = false                  # true = disable auth entirely (DANGEROUS if exposed)
    36→                                  # When disabled, anyone with network access can use moltis
    37→
    38→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    39→# GRAPHQL
    40→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    41→
    42→[graphql]
    43→enabled = false                   # Enable GraphQL endpoint (/graphql for HTTP + WebSocket)
    44→                                  # Can be toggled at runtime in Settings > GraphQL
    45→
    46→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    47→# TLS / HTTPS
    48→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    49→
    50→[tls]
    51→enabled = true                    # Enable HTTPS (recommended)
    52→auto_generate = true              # Auto-generate local CA and server certificate
    53→# http_redirect_port = 18790      # Optional override (default: server.port + 1)
    54→# cert_path = "/path/to/cert.pem"     # Custom certificate file (overrides auto-gen)
    55→# key_path = "/path/to/key.pem"       # Custom private key file
    56→# ca_cert_path = "/path/to/ca.pem"    # CA certificate for trust instructions
    57→
    58→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    59→# AGENT IDENTITY
    60→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    61→# Customize your agent's personality. These are typically set during onboarding.
    62→
    63→[identity]
    64→# name = "moltis"                 # Agent's display name
    65→# emoji = "🦊"                    # Agent's emoji/avatar
    66→# theme = "wise owl"              # Theme for agent personality (e.g. wise owl, chill fox)
    67→# soul = ""                       # Freeform personality text injected into system prompt
    68→                                  # Use this for custom instructions, tone, or behavior
    69→
    70→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    71→# USER PROFILE
    72→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    73→# Information about you. Set during onboarding.
    74→
    75→[user]
    76→# name = "Your Name"              # Your name (used in conversations)
    77→# timezone = "America/New_York"   # Your timezone (IANA format)
    78→
    79→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    80→# LLM PROVIDERS

    80→# LLM PROVIDERS
    81→# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    82→# Configure API keys and settings for each LLM provider.
    83→# API keys can also be set via environment variables (preferred for security).

    87→#   api_key   - API key (or use env var like ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)
    88→#   base_url  - Override API endpoint
    89→#   models    - Preferred models shown first (optional)
    90→#   fetch_models - Discover models from provider API when available (default: true)
    91→#   stream_transport - Streaming transport: "sse", "websocket", or "auto" (default: "sse")
    92→#   alias     - Custom name for metrics labels (useful for multiple instances)

    95→offered = ["local-llm", "github-copilot", "openai-codex", "openai", "anthropic", "openrouter", "ollama", "moonshot", "minimax", "zai"] # Enabled providers and those shown in onboarding/picker UI ([] = enable/show all)

  1038→fn check_semantic_warnings(config: &MoltisConfig, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
  1039→    let is_localhost = config.server.bind == "127.0.0.1"
  1040→        || config.server.bind == "localhost"
  1041→        || config.server.bind == "::1";
  1042→
  1043→    // auth.disabled + non-localhost
  1044→    if config.auth.disabled && !is_localhost {
  1045→        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic {
  1046→            severity: Severity::Warning,
  1047→            category: "security",
  1048→            path: "auth".into(),
  1049→            message: format!(
  1050→                "authentication is disabled while binding to {}",
  1051→                config.server.bind
  1052→            ),
  1053→        });
  1054→    }
  1055→
  1056→    // TLS disabled + non-localhost
  1057→    if !config.tls.enabled && !is_localhost {
  1058→        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic {
  1059→            severity: Severity::Warning,
  1060→            category: "security",
  1061→            path: "tls".into(),
  1062→            message: format!("TLS is disabled while binding to {}", config.server.bind),
  1063→        });
  1064→    }
  1065→
  1066→    // TLS cert without key or vice versa
  1067→    let has_cert = config.tls.cert_path.is_some();

  1073→            path: "tls".into(),
  1074→            message: "tls.cert_path is set but tls.key_path is missing".into(),
  1075→        });
  1076→    }
  1077→    if has_key && !has_cert {

  1082→            message: "tls.key_path is set but tls.cert_path is missing".into(),
  1083→        });
  1084→    }

  1093→        });
  1094→    }

     1→use std::sync::Arc;
     2→
     3→use {
     4→    secrecy::ExposeSecret,
     5→    teloxide::{
     6→        ApiError, RequestError,
     7→        prelude::*,
     8→        types::{AllowedUpdate, BotCommand, UpdateKind},
     9→    },
    10→    tokio_util::sync::CancellationToken,
    11→    tracing::{debug, error, info, warn},
    12→};
    13→
    14→use moltis_channels::{ChannelEventSink, message_log::MessageLog};
    15→
    16→use crate::{
    17→    config::TelegramAccountConfig,
    18→    handlers,
    19→    outbound::TelegramOutbound,
    20→    state::{AccountState, AccountStateMap},
    21→};
    22→
    23→/// Start polling for a single bot account.
    24→///
    25→/// Spawns a background task that processes updates until the returned
    26→/// `CancellationToken` is cancelled.
    27→pub async fn start_polling(
    28→    account_id: String,
    29→    config: TelegramAccountConfig,
    30→    accounts: AccountStateMap,
    31→    message_log: Option<Arc<dyn MessageLog>>,
    32→    event_sink: Option<Arc<dyn ChannelEventSink>>,
    33→) -> crate::Result<CancellationToken> {
    34→    // Build bot with a client timeout longer than the long-polling timeout (30s)
    35→    // so the HTTP client doesn't abort the request before Telegram responds.
    36→    let client = teloxide::net::default_reqwest_settings()
    37→        .timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(45))
    38→        .build()
    39→        .map_err(|source| crate::Error::external("build telegram client", source))?;
    40→    let bot = Bot::with_client(config.token.expose_secret(), client);
    41→
    42→    // Verify credentials and get bot username.
    43→    let me = bot.get_me().await?;
    44→    let bot_username = me.username.clone();
    45→
    46→    // Delete any existing webhook so long polling works.
    47→    bot.delete_webhook().send().await?;
    48→
    49→    // Register slash commands for autocomplete in Telegram clients.
    50→    let commands = vec![
    51→        BotCommand::new("new", "Start a new session"),
    52→        BotCommand::new("sessions", "List and switch sessions"),
    53→        BotCommand::new("agent", "Switch session agent"),
    54→        BotCommand::new("model", "Switch provider/model"),
    55→        BotCommand::new("sandbox", "Toggle sandbox and choose image"),
    56→        BotCommand::new("sh", "Enable shell command mode"),
    57→        BotCommand::new("clear", "Clear session history"),
    58→        BotCommand::new("compact", "Compact session (summarize)"),
    59→        BotCommand::new("context", "Show session context info"),
    60→        BotCommand::new("help", "Show available commands"),
    61→    ];
    62→    if let Err(e) = bot.set_my_commands(commands).await {
    63→        warn!(account_id, "failed to register bot commands: {e}");
    64→    }
    65→
    66→    info!(
    67→        account_id,
    68→        username = ?bot_username,
    69→        "telegram bot connected (webhook cleared)"
    70→    );
    71→
    72→    let cancel = CancellationToken::new();
    73→
    74→    let outbound = Arc::new(TelegramOutbound {
    75→        accounts: Arc::clone(&accounts),
    76→    });
    77→
    78→    let otp_cooldown = config.otp_cooldown_secs;
    79→    let state = AccountState {
    80→        bot: bot.clone(),
    81→        bot_username,
    82→        account_id: account_id.clone(),
    83→        config,
    84→        outbound,
    85→        cancel: cancel.clone(),
    86→        message_log,
    87→        event_sink,
    88→        otp: std::sync::Mutex::new(crate::otp::OtpState::new(otp_cooldown)),
    89→    };
    90→
    91→    {
    92→        let mut map = accounts.write().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    93→        map.insert(account_id.clone(), state);
    94→    }
    95→
    96→    let cancel_clone = cancel.clone();
    97→    let aid = account_id.clone();
    98→    let poll_accounts = Arc::clone(&accounts);
    99→    tokio::spawn(async move {
   100→        info!(account_id = aid, "starting telegram manual polling loop");
   101→        let mut offset: i32 = 0;
   102→
   103→        loop {
   104→            if cancel_clone.is_cancelled() {
   105→                info!(account_id = aid, "telegram polling stopped");
   106→                break;
   107→            }
   108→
   109→            let result = bot
   110→                .get_updates()
   111→                .offset(offset)
   112→                .timeout(30)
   113→                .allowed_updates(vec![
   114→                    AllowedUpdate::Message,
   115→                    AllowedUpdate::EditedMessage,
   116→                    AllowedUpdate::CallbackQuery,
   117→                ])
   118→                .await;
   119→
   120→            match result {
   121→                Ok(updates) => {
   122→                    debug!(
   123→                        account_id = aid,
   124→                        count = updates.len(),
   125→                        "got telegram updates"
   126→                    );
   127→                    for update in updates {
   128→                        offset = update.id.as_offset();
   129→                        match update.kind {
   130→                            UpdateKind::Message(msg) => {
   131→                                debug!(
   132→                                    account_id = aid,
   133→                                    chat_id = msg.chat.id.0,
   134→                                    "received telegram message"
   135→                                );
   136→                                if let Err(e) =
   137→                                    handlers::handle_message_direct(msg, &bot, &aid, &poll_accounts)
   138→                                        .await
   139→                                {
   140→                                    error!(
   141→                                        account_id = aid,
   142→                                        error = %e,
   143→                                        "error handling telegram message"
   144→                                    );
   145→                                }
   146→                            },
   147→                            UpdateKind::EditedMessage(msg) => {
   148→                                debug!(
   149→                                    account_id = aid,
   150→                                    chat_id = msg.chat.id.0,
   151→                                    "received telegram edited message"
   152→                                );
   153→                                if let Err(e) =
   154→                                    handlers::handle_edited_location(msg, &aid, &poll_accounts)
   155→                                        .await
   156→                                {
   157→                                    error!(
   158→                                        account_id = aid,
   159→                                        error = %e,
   160→                                        "error handling telegram edited message"
   161→                                    );
   162→                                }
   163→                            },
   164→                            UpdateKind::CallbackQuery(query) => {
   165→                                debug!(
   166→                                    account_id = aid,
   167→                                    callback_data = ?query.data,
   168→                                    "received telegram callback query"
   169→                                );
   170→                                if let Err(e) = handlers::handle_callback_query(
   171→                                    query,
   172→                                    &bot,
   173→                                    &aid,
   174→                                    &poll_accounts,
   175→                                )
   176→                                .await
   177→                                {
   178→                                    error!(
   179→                                        account_id = aid,
   180→                                        error = %e,
   181→                                        "error handling telegram callback query"
   182→                                    );
   183→                                }
   184→                            },
   185→                            other => {
   186→                                debug!(account_id = aid, "ignoring non-message update: {other:?}");
   187→                            },
   188→                        }
   189→                    }
   190→                },
   191→                Err(e) => {
   192→                    // Detect conflict error: another bot instance is running with the same token.
   193→                    let is_conflict =
   194→                        matches!(&e, RequestError::Api(ApiError::TerminatedByOtherGetUpdates));
   195→
   196→                    if is_conflict {
   197→                        warn!(
   198→                            account_id = aid,
   199→                            "telegram bot disabled: another instance is already running with this token"
   200→                        );
   201→
   202→                        // Request the gateway to disable this channel.
   203→                        let event_sink = {
   204→                            let accounts = poll_accounts.read().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
   205→                            accounts.get(&aid).and_then(|s| s.event_sink.clone())
   206→                        };
   207→                        if let Some(sink) = event_sink {
   208→                            sink.request_disable_account(
   209→                                "telegram",
   210→                                &aid,
   211→                                "Another bot instance is already running with this token",
   212→                            )
   213→                            .await;
   214→                        }
   215→
   216→                        // Cancel this polling loop and exit.
   217→                        cancel_clone.cancel();
   218→                        break;
   219→                    }
   220→
   221→                    warn!(account_id = aid, error = %e, "telegram getUpdates failed");
   222→                    tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)).await;
   223→                },
   224→            }
   225→        }
   226→    });
   227→
   228→    Ok(cancel)
   229→}
   230→
```

  "new_string": "static SHARED_CLIENT: std::sync::OnceLock<reqwest::Client> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Initialize the shared provider HTTP client with optional upstream proxy.\n///\n/// Call once at gateway startup; subsequent calls are no-ops.\npub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {\n    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(moltis_tools::build_http_client(proxy_url));\n}\n\n/// Shared HTTP client for LLM providers.\n///\n/// All providers that don't need custom redirect/proxy settings should\n/// reuse this client to share connection pools, DNS cache, and TLS sessions.\n///\n/// Falls back to a plain client if [`init_shared_http_client`] was never\n/// called (e.g. in tests).\npub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {\n    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)\n}"
}

Now add the `http_client` module to `moltis-common`:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01CzRr1eQYiaSvARP1BaFU6n
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/common/src/http_client.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared HTTP client builder with optional upstream proxy.\n//!\n//! All crates that need a `reqwest::Client` with proxy support should use\n//! [`build_http_client`] rather than building their own, to keep proxy\n//! handling consistent.\n\n/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] with optional upstream proxy.\n///\n/// When `proxy_url` is `Some`, all requests are routed through that proxy\n/// (HTTP CONNECT for HTTPS targets, forward-proxy for plain HTTP).\n/// Localhost/loopback addresses are automatically excluded via `no_proxy`.\n///\n/// Supports `http://`, `https://`, `socks5://`, and `socks5h://` schemes.\npub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {\n    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();\n    if let Some(url) = proxy_url\n        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)\n    {\n        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string(\"localhost,127.0.0.1,::1\"));\n        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n    }\n    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn no_proxy_returns_default_client() {\n        let client = build_http_client(None);\n        // Smoke test: the client was created without error.\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn valid_http_proxy() {\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"http://127.0.0.1:8080\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn valid_socks5_proxy() {\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"socks5://127.0.0.1:1080\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn invalid_proxy_url_falls_back() {\n        // Garbage URL: should fall back to a plain client, not panic.\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"not-a-url\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/tools/src/lib.rs",
  "old_string": "static SHARED_CLIENT: std::sync::OnceLock<reqwest::Client> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Initialize the shared HTTP client with optional proxy.\n/// Call once at gateway startup; subsequent calls are no-ops.\npub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {\n    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(build_http_client(proxy_url));\n}\n\n/// Shared HTTP client for tools that don't need custom configuration.\n///\n/// Reusing a single `reqwest::Client` avoids per-request connection pool,\n/// DNS resolver, and TLS session cache overhead — significant on\n/// memory-constrained devices.\n///\n/// Falls back to a plain client if [`init_shared_http_client`] was never\n/// called (e.g. in tests).\npub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {\n    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)\n}\n\n/// Build a `reqwest::Client` with optional proxy configuration.\npub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {\n    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();\n    if let Some(url) = proxy_url\n        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)\n    {\n        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string(\"localhost,127.0.0.1,::1\"));\n        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n    }\n    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())\n}",
  "new_string": "static SHARED_CLIENT: std::sync::OnceLock<reqwest::Client> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Initialize the shared HTTP client with optional proxy.\n/// Call once at gateway startup; subsequent calls are no-ops.\npub fn init_shared_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) {\n    let _ = SHARED_CLIENT.set(moltis_common::http_client::build_http_client(proxy_url));\n}\n\n/// Shared HTTP client for tools that don't need custom configuration.\n///\n/// Reusing a single `reqwest::Client` avoids per-request connection pool,\n/// DNS resolver, and TLS session cache overhead — significant on\n/// memory-constrained devices.\n///\n/// Falls back to a plain client if [`init_shared_http_client`] was never\n/// called (e.g. in tests).\npub fn shared_http_client() -> &'static reqwest::Client {\n    SHARED_CLIENT.get_or_init(reqwest::Client::new)\n}\n\n/// Build a `reqwest::Client` with optional proxy configuration.\n///\n/// Re-export of [`moltis_common::http_client::build_http_client`] for\n/// backward compatibility.\npub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {\n    moltis_common::http_client::build_http_client(proxy_url)\n}"
}

> AGENT

The best approach: Initialize the upstream proxy **before** the trusted-network block. The trusted-network proxy is for sandbox domain filtering and uses a local address. The upstream proxy is the user's corporate/network proxy. When both are active, tools should chain through the sandbox proxy (which already intercepts), but the **providers** and **channels** (which bypass the sandbox proxy) should use the upstream proxy directly.

Let me refine the approach:
- `moltis_providers::init_shared_http_client` → always gets the upstream proxy
- `moltis_tools::init_shared_http_client` → gets the sandbox proxy when trusted mode is on, else gets the upstream proxy
- Channels → get the upstream proxy

The simplest approach for channels: since `moltis_common::http_client::build_http_client` is available everywhere, channels can just call it directly with the proxy URL. But they need to know the proxy URL. Rather than threading it through every plugin API, I'll use a global accessor pattern like the shared clients.

Let me add a `upstream_proxy_url()` accessor to `moltis-common`:

{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/common/src/http_client.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared HTTP client builder with optional upstream proxy.\n//!\n//! All crates that need a `reqwest::Client` with proxy support should use\n//! [`build_http_client`] rather than building their own, to keep proxy\n//! handling consistent.\n//!\n//! The gateway calls [`set_upstream_proxy`] once at startup so that any\n//! crate can later retrieve the URL via [`upstream_proxy_url`] without\n//! needing it threaded through every constructor.\n\nstatic UPSTREAM_PROXY: std::sync::OnceLock<String> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Store the user-configured upstream proxy URL (call once at startup).\npub fn set_upstream_proxy(url: &str) {\n    let _ = UPSTREAM_PROXY.set(url.to_string());\n}\n\n/// Return the upstream proxy URL, if one was configured.\npub fn upstream_proxy_url() -> Option<&'static str> {\n    UPSTREAM_PROXY.get().map(String::as_str)\n}\n\n/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] with optional upstream proxy.\n///\n/// When `proxy_url` is `Some`, all requests are routed through that proxy\n/// (HTTP CONNECT for HTTPS targets, forward-proxy for plain HTTP).\n/// Localhost/loopback addresses are automatically excluded via `no_proxy`.\n///\n/// Supports `http://`, `https://`, `socks5://`, and `socks5h://` schemes.\npub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {\n    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();\n    if let Some(url) = proxy_url\n        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)\n    {\n        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string(\"localhost,127.0.0.1,::1\"));\n        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n    }\n    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())\n}\n\n/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] using the globally configured upstream proxy.\n///\n/// Convenience wrapper around [`build_http_client`] + [`upstream_proxy_url`].\npub fn build_default_http_client() -> reqwest::Client {\n    build_http_client(upstream_proxy_url())\n}\n\n/// Apply the upstream proxy to an existing [`reqwest::ClientBuilder`].\n///\n/// Useful when callers need additional builder options (timeout, redirect\n/// policy, etc.) but still want the global proxy applied.\npub fn apply_proxy(mut builder: reqwest::ClientBuilder) -> reqwest::ClientBuilder {\n    if let Some(url) = upstream_proxy_url()\n        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)\n    {\n        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string(\"localhost,127.0.0.1,::1\"));\n        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n    }\n    builder\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn no_proxy_returns_default_client() {\n        let client = build_http_client(None);\n        // Smoke test: the client was created without error.\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn valid_http_proxy() {\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"http://127.0.0.1:8080\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn valid_socks5_proxy() {\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"socks5://127.0.0.1:1080\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn invalid_proxy_url_falls_back() {\n        // Garbage URL: should fall back to a plain client, not panic.\n        let client = build_http_client(Some(\"not-a-url\"));\n        drop(client);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn apply_proxy_with_none_is_passthrough() {\n        // When no upstream proxy is set, apply_proxy is a no-op.\n        let builder = reqwest::Client::builder().timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(10));\n        let builder = apply_proxy(builder);\n        let client = builder.build().unwrap();\n        drop(client);\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

The issue is that `teloxide` uses reqwest 0.11 internally, so `Bot::with_client` expects a reqwest 0.11 `Client`, while `build_http_client` returns a reqwest 0.12 `Client`. The `apply_proxy` function won't work here because the builder types are different.

> AGENT

So `teloxide-core` uses reqwest 0.11 internally. The `Bot::with_client` method expects reqwest 0.11's `Client`. We can't use our reqwest 0.12 builder there.

For Telegram, I need to use `teloxide::net::default_reqwest_settings()` which returns a reqwest 0.11 builder, and apply the proxy manually using the 0.11 API. But `reqwest::Proxy` is from 0.12 in our workspace...

The cleanest solution: teloxide supports the `HTTPS_PROXY` env var natively. Since `teloxide-core`'s reqwest 0.11 respects system proxy env vars, we can set the env var before starting the bot. But that's global and messy.

Actually, the better approach: revert the Telegram change, and instead document that for Telegram, the `HTTPS_PROXY` env var must be used (which teloxide's bundled reqwest 0.11 respects). This is a known limitation of the teloxide version pinning.

```
//! Network-specific API.

use std::time::Duration;

pub use self::download::{download_file, download_file_stream, Download};

pub(crate) use self::{
    request::{request_json, request_multipart},
    telegram_response::TelegramResponse,
};

mod download;
mod request;
mod telegram_response;

/// The default Telegram API URL.
pub const TELEGRAM_API_URL: &str = "https://api.telegram.org";

/// Constructs a network client from the `TELOXIDE_PROXY` environmental
/// variable.
///
/// This function passes the value of `TELOXIDE_PROXY` into
/// [`reqwest::Proxy::all`], if it exists, otherwise returns the default
/// client.
///
/// ## Note
///
/// The created client will have safe settings, meaning that it will be able to
/// work in long time durations, see the [issue 223].
///
/// [`reqwest::Proxy::all`]: https://docs.rs/reqwest/latest/reqwest/struct.Proxy.html#method.all
/// [issue 223]: https://github.com/teloxide/teloxide/issues/223
///
/// ## Panics
///
/// If `TELOXIDE_PROXY` exists, but isn't correct url.
#[must_use]
pub fn client_from_env() -> reqwest::Client {
    use reqwest::Proxy;

    const TELOXIDE_PROXY: &str = "TELOXIDE_PROXY";

    let builder = default_reqwest_settings();

    match std::env::var(TELOXIDE_PROXY).ok() {
        Some(proxy) => builder.proxy(Proxy::all(proxy).expect("reqwest::Proxy creation failed")),
        None => builder,
    }
    .build()
    .expect("creating reqwest::Client")
}

/// Returns a reqwest client builder with default settings.
///
/// Client built from default settings is supposed to work over long time
/// durations, see the [issue 223].
///
/// The current settings are:
///  - A connection timeout of 5 seconds.
///  - A timeout of 17 seconds.
///  - `tcp_nodelay` is on.
///
/// ## Notes
///
/// 1. The settings may change in the future.
/// 2. If you are using the polling mechanism to get updates, the timeout
///    configured in the client should be bigger than the polling timeout.
/// 3. If you alter the current settings listed above, your bot will not be
///    guaranteed to work over long time durations.
///
/// [issue 223]: https://github.com/teloxide/teloxide/issues/223
pub fn default_reqwest_settings() -> reqwest::ClientBuilder {
    reqwest::Client::builder()
        .connect_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5))
        .timeout(Duration::from_secs(17))
        .tcp_nodelay(true)
}

/// Creates URL for making HTTPS requests. See the [Telegram documentation].
///
/// [Telegram documentation]: https://core.telegram.org/bots/api#making-requests
fn method_url(base: reqwest::Url, token: &str, method_name: &str) -> reqwest::Url {
    base.join(&format!("/bot{token}/{method_name}")).expect("failed to format url")
}

/// Creates URL for downloading a file. See the [Telegram documentation].
///
/// [Telegram documentation]: https://core.telegram.org/bots/api#file
fn file_url(base: reqwest::Url, token: &str, file_path: &str) -> reqwest::Url {
    base.join(&format!("file/bot{token}/{file_path}")).expect("failed to format url")
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use crate::net::*;

    #[test]
    fn method_url_test() {
        let url = method_url(
            reqwest::Url::parse(TELEGRAM_API_URL).unwrap(),
            "535362388:REDACTED",
            "methodName",
        );

        assert_eq!(
            url.as_str(),
            "https://api.telegram.org/bot535362388:REDACTED/methodName"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn file_url_test() {
        let url = file_url(
            reqwest::Url::parse(TELEGRAM_API_URL).unwrap(),
            "535362388:REDACTED",
            "AgADAgADyqoxG2g8aEsu_KjjVsGF4-zetw8ABAEAAwIAA20AA_8QAwABFgQ",
        );

        assert_eq!(
            url.as_str(),
            "https://api.telegram.org/file/bot535362388:REDACTED/AgADAgADyqoxG2g8aEsu_KjjVsGF4-zetw8ABAEAAwIAA20AA_8QAwABFgQ"
        );
    }
}
```

OK so there's only `client_from_env()` and `default_reqwest_settings()`. `client_from_env()` creates the client with `TELOXIDE_PROXY` but uses the default 17s timeout, and we need 45s. I need to replicate the proxy logic manually using the 0.11 reqwest API. But we can't import reqwest 0.11 directly since our workspace uses 0.12.

The simplest safe approach: just use `default_reqwest_settings()` (which returns a reqwest 0.11 `ClientBuilder`) and manually check `TELOXIDE_PROXY`:

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/telegram/src/bot.rs",
  "old_string": "    // Build bot with a client timeout longer than the long-polling timeout (30s)\n    // so the HTTP client doesn't abort the request before Telegram responds.\n    //\n    // NOTE: teloxide bundles reqwest 0.11 internally. The upstream proxy is\n    // applied via `TELOXIDE_PROXY` env var set at gateway startup, which\n    // `teloxide::net::client_from_env()` reads. We override the timeout\n    // on the builder returned by `default_reqwest_settings()` + use\n    // `client_from_env()` to pick up the proxy.\n    let client = teloxide::net::client_from_env_with_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(45));",
  "new_string": "    // Build bot with a client timeout longer than the long-polling timeout (30s)\n    // so the HTTP client doesn't abort the request before Telegram responds.\n    //\n    // Upstream proxy: teloxide bundles reqwest 0.11, so we cannot inject our\n    // reqwest 0.12 proxy object. The gateway sets `TELOXIDE_PROXY` env var at\n    // startup when `upstream_proxy` is configured; teloxide picks it up via\n    // `client_from_env()`. We replicate that logic here with a longer timeout.\n    let mut builder = teloxide::net::default_reqwest_settings()\n        .timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(45));\n    if let Ok(proxy_url) = std::env::var(\"TELOXIDE_PROXY\") {\n        if let Ok(proxy) = teloxide::net::private_hack_proxy_all(&proxy_url) {\n            builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n        }\n    }\n    let client = builder\n        .build()\n        .map_err(|source| crate::Error::external(\"build telegram client\", source))?;"

Actually, wait. Let me reconsider. The `#[tokio::main]` on `main` doesn't mean multiple threads already run when main begins. The macro starts the runtime, and initially only the main task runs. Other tasks only spawn when explicitly spawned. The config is loaded and the gateway starts before channels are initialized. tool_use Write
id: toolu_01AUU9vVa3akSHnsx9eayo3k
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/docs/src/upstream-proxy.md",
  "content": "# Upstream Proxy\n\nMoltis can route all outbound HTTP traffic through an upstream proxy. This is\nuseful when running behind a corporate firewall, in a restricted network, or\nwhen you need to audit/filter outbound connections.\n\n## Configuration\n\nAdd `upstream_proxy` to the top level of your `moltis.toml`:\n\n```toml\nupstream_proxy = \"http://proxy.corp.example.com:8080\"\n```\n\n### Supported schemes\n\n| Scheme | Example | Notes |\n|--------|---------|-------|\n| `http://` | `http://proxy:8080` | HTTP CONNECT proxy (most common) |\n| `https://` | `https://proxy:8443` | TLS-encrypted proxy connection |\n| `socks5://` | `socks5://proxy:1080` | SOCKS5 proxy (DNS resolved locally) |\n| `socks5h://` | `socks5h://proxy:1080` | SOCKS5 proxy (DNS resolved by proxy) |\n\n### Proxy authentication\n\nInclude credentials in the URL:\n\n```toml\nupstream_proxy = \"http://user:password@proxy.corp.example.com:8080\"\n```\n\n### What is proxied\n\nWhen `upstream_proxy` is set, the following traffic routes through the proxy:\n\n- **LLM provider API calls** (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, etc.)\n- **Tool HTTP requests** (web fetch, web search, Firecrawl)\n- **OAuth flows** (device auth, token exchange)\n- **MCP server auth** (OAuth for remote MCP servers)\n- **Channel outbound** (Slack API calls, MS Teams API calls)\n\nLocalhost and loopback addresses (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`) are\nautomatically excluded from the proxy (`no_proxy`).\n\n### Telegram caveat\n\nTelegram uses [teloxide](https://github.com/teloxide/teloxide) which bundles\nits own HTTP client (reqwest 0.11). The `upstream_proxy` config does not apply\nto Telegram traffic directly. To proxy Telegram, set the standard\n`HTTPS_PROXY` environment variable, which teloxide's reqwest honours:\n\n```bash\nexport HTTPS_PROXY=http://proxy.corp.example.com:8080\nmoltis\n```\n\nOr use the `env` section in `moltis.toml`:\n\n```toml\n[env]\nHTTPS_PROXY = \"http://proxy.corp.example.com:8080\"\n```\n\n## Environment variable fallback\n\nWhen `upstream_proxy` is **not** set in `moltis.toml`, reqwest automatically\nhonours the standard proxy environment variables:\n\n- `HTTP_PROXY` / `http_proxy`\n- `HTTPS_PROXY` / `https_proxy`\n- `ALL_PROXY` / `all_proxy`\n- `NO_PROXY` / `no_proxy`\n\nSetting `upstream_proxy` in the config takes precedence over these variables\nfor all traffic except Telegram (see caveat above).\n\n## Interaction with Trusted Network\n\nIf you use both `upstream_proxy` and\n[trusted network mode](trusted-network.md) (`network = \"trusted\"`), they\nserve different purposes:\n\n- **Trusted network proxy** is a local domain-filtering proxy for sandbox\n  tool execution. It controls *which* domains tools can reach.\n- **Upstream proxy** routes traffic through your corporate/network proxy to\n  *reach* the internet.\n\nWhen both are active, tool traffic routes through the trusted-network proxy\n(which enforces domain allowlists), while provider and channel traffic routes\nthrough the upstream proxy.\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Committed. Here's a summary of what was implemented:

## Summary

**23 files changed, 341 insertions, 26 deletions**

### What's covered

| Component | Proxy applied via |
|-----------|------------------|
| LLM Providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, etc.) | `moltis_providers::shared_http_client()` |
| Tools (web_fetch, web_search, firecrawl) | `moltis_tools::shared_http_client()` + `web_fetch` fallback |
| OAuth flows | `moltis_common::http_client::build_default_http_client()` |
| MCP server auth | Same |
| Slack outbound | Same |
| MS Teams outbound | Same |

### Known limitation

**Telegram**: teloxide bundles reqwest 0.11 internally, and the workspace uses reqwest 0.12. We can't inject a 0.12 `Proxy` object into 0.11's `ClientBuilder`. Users should set `HTTPS_PROXY` env var (which teloxide's reqwest 0.11 honors) or use the `[env]` config section. This is documented in the new `docs/src/upstream-proxy.md` page.

### Key architectural decisions

- `build_http_client()` lives in `moltis-common` (shared by both `moltis-tools` and `moltis-providers`)
- Global `upstream_proxy_url()` accessor avoids threading the URL through every plugin/channel constructor
- When both upstream proxy and trusted-network proxy are active, tools use the sandbox proxy while providers/channels use the upstream proxy

> AGENT

  "command": "gh pr create --title \"feat(config): add upstream_proxy for application-level HTTP proxy support\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Add `upstream_proxy` top-level config field to `moltis.toml` that routes all outbound HTTP traffic through a user-configured proxy (http/https/socks5/socks5h)\n- Create `moltis_common::http_client` module as the single source of truth for proxy-aware HTTP client construction\n- Wire the proxy through providers, tools, channels (Slack, Teams), OAuth, and MCP auth at gateway startup\n\nCloses #548.\n\n### What is proxied\n\n| Component | Method |\n|-----------|--------|\n| LLM providers (Anthropic, OpenAI, etc.) | `moltis_providers::shared_http_client()` initialized at startup |\n| Tools (web_fetch, web_search, firecrawl) | `moltis_tools::shared_http_client()` + per-request fallback |\n| OAuth flows | `build_default_http_client()` |\n| MCP server auth | `build_default_http_client()` |\n| Slack outbound | `build_default_http_client()` |\n| MS Teams outbound | `build_default_http_client()` |\n| Telegram | ⚠️ Requires `HTTPS_PROXY` env var (teloxide bundles reqwest 0.11) |\n\n### Known limitation\n\nTelegram uses teloxide which bundles reqwest 0.11 internally. Since our workspace uses reqwest 0.12, we cannot inject a proxy object directly. Users should set `HTTPS_PROXY` env var or use the `[env]` config section. Documented in `docs/src/upstream-proxy.md`.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Completed\n\n- [x] `cargo check` passes\n- [x] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 fmt --all -- --check` passes\n- [x] Clippy passes (macOS triple: workspace excluding providers/gateway, then providers and gateway separately with `--features local-llm-metal`)\n- [x] `taplo fmt` applied\n- [x] `cargo fetch --locked` passes\n- [x] All `moltis-common`, `moltis-config`, `moltis-tools`, `moltis-providers` tests pass (1285 tests)\n- [x] New tests: `upstream_proxy_not_flagged_as_unknown`, `upstream_proxy_invalid_scheme_rejected`, `upstream_proxy_valid_schemes_accepted`, `http_client::*` (5 tests)\n\n### Remaining\n\n- [ ] `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR_NUMBER>` (full validation with PR number)\n- [ ] Manual QA: set `upstream_proxy` in moltis.toml and verify provider API calls route through proxy\n\n## Manual QA\n\n1. Add `upstream_proxy = \"http://127.0.0.1:8080\"` to `moltis.toml`\n2. Start moltis, observe log line: `upstream proxy configured for providers and channels`\n3. Send a chat message — verify the provider HTTP request routes through the proxy\n4. Test with an invalid scheme (e.g. `ftp://...`) — `moltis config check` should report an error\n5. Remove the field — verify `HTTPS_PROXY` env var fallback works (reqwest default behavior)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",

  "timeout": 30000
}

  "new_string": "    // upstream_proxy: must be a valid URL with a supported scheme.\n    if let Some(ref proxy) = config.upstream_proxy {\n        let url = proxy.expose_secret();\n        let valid = url.starts_with(\"http://\")\n            || url.starts_with(\"https://\")\n            || url.starts_with(\"socks5://\")\n            || url.starts_with(\"socks5h://\");\n        if !valid {\n            // Extract only the scheme portion (before \"://\") to avoid leaking\n            // credentials that may be embedded in the URL.\n            let scheme = url.split(\"://\").next().unwrap_or(\"<unknown>\");\n            diagnostics.push(Diagnostic {\n                severity: Severity::Error,\n                category: \"invalid-value\",\n                path: \"upstream_proxy\".into(),\n                message: format!(\n                    \"upstream_proxy must use http://, https://, socks5://, or socks5h:// scheme (got \\\"{scheme}://\\\")\"\n                ),\n            });\n        }\n    }"
}

{

  "old_string": "    }\n    builder\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]",
  "new_string": "    }\n    builder\n}\n\n/// Redact credentials from a proxy URL for safe logging.\n///\n/// Returns a version of the URL with `user:pass@` replaced by `***@`.\n/// If the URL cannot be parsed, returns `\"<invalid proxy URL>\"`.\npub fn redact_proxy_url(url: &str) -> String {\n    // Parse minimally: look for `://user:pass@` pattern.\n    if let Some(scheme_end) = url.find(\"://\") {\n        let after_scheme = &url[scheme_end + 3..];\n        if let Some(at_pos) = after_scheme.find('@') {\n            // Has userinfo — redact it.\n            let host_part = &after_scheme[at_pos..]; // includes '@'\n            return format!(\"{}://***{host_part}\", &url[..scheme_end]);\n        }\n    }\n    // No credentials or unparseable — return as-is (no secrets to leak).\n    url.to_string()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]"
}

> AGENT

Pushed. Both review comments are addressed:

1. **P1 (credentials in logs)**: Added `redact_proxy_url()` that strips `user:pass@` to `***@` before logging. Validation errors also only show the scheme, not the full URL.

2. **P2 (Secret wrapping)**: Changed `upstream_proxy` to `Option<Secret<String>>` with the same serde helpers used by other secret fields. `expose_secret()` is called only at consumption points (gateway startup, passing to reqwest).

{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P1\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **Proxy credentials exposed in logs**\n\nIf `upstream_proxy` is set with embedded credentials (e.g. `http://user:password@proxy:8080`), the full URL — including the password — is emitted at `info!` level and will appear in any log aggregator, terminal, or monitoring pipeline.\n\nAt minimum, suppress the URL entirely:\n```suggestion\n        info!(\"upstream proxy configured for providers and channels\");\n```\n\nOr redact the userinfo before logging:\n```rust\nlet display = url::Url::parse(url).ok().map(|mut u| {\n    let _ = u.set_password(None);\n    let _ = u.set_username(\"\");\n    u.to_string()\n}).unwrap_or_else(|| \"\u003cinvalid URL\u003e\".into());\ninfo!(upstream_proxy = %display, \"upstream proxy configured for providers and channels\");\n```","id":3036995515,"line":null,"path":"crates/gateway/src/server.rs","user":"greptile-apps[bot]"}
{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P2\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **Proxy URL should use `Secret\u003cString\u003e`**\n\n`upstream_proxy` can carry credentials in the URL (e.g. `http://user:pass@proxy:8080`). Per the project security policy, passwords and tokens must be stored as `secrecy::Secret\u003cString\u003e` so the value is redacted in derived `Debug` output and cannot be accidentally printed. All call sites would call `.expose_secret()` only at the consumption point, consistent with `CredentialStore` and `OAuthConfig`.\n\n```suggestion\n    pub upstream_proxy: Option\u003csecrecy::Secret\u003cString\u003e\u003e,\n```\n\n**Context Used:** CLAUDE.md ([source](https://app.greptile.com/review/custom-context?memory=45affda4-4925-4beb-9421-4644ac793b0e))","id":3036995541,"line":null,"path":"crates/config/src/schema.rs","user":"greptile-apps[bot]"}

Both prior P1 concerns are fully resolved: `upstream_proxy` is stored as `Secret\u003cString\u003e` in the config schema and credentials are redacted before any log emission.\n\n\u003ch3\u003eConfidence Score: 5/5\u003c/h3\u003e\n\nSafe to merge — both prior P1 concerns (Secret\u003cString\u003e storage and credential logging) are fully addressed; all remaining findings are P2 style suggestions\n\nThe two previously flagged P1 issues have been properly resolved: upstream_proxy is wrapped in Secret\u003cString\u003e in the config schema and credentials are redacted via redact_proxy_url before any log output. None of these affect correctness for the common case.\n\ncrates/common/src/http_client.rs (rfind fix for redact_proxy_url, warn! on proxy fallback) and docs/src/upstream-proxy.md (Slack partial-proxy caveat)\n\n\u003ch3\u003eImportant Files Changed\u003c/h3\u003e\n\n\n\n\n| Filename | Overview |\n|----------|----------|\n| crates/common/src/http_client.rs | New centralized proxy-aware HTTP client module; No new review comments since the last batch. The Greptile summary comment mentions three remaining P2 items:

1. **`redact_proxy_url` uses `find('@')` instead of `rfind('@')`** — if the password contains a literal `@` (e.g. `p%40ss` decoded), `find` would mis-redact
2. **`build_http_client` silently falls back** on bad URL with no log
3. **Slack `chat.postMessage` uses `slack-morphism`'s hyper connector** which bypasses the proxy — undocumented

Let me fix all three.

     1→//! Shared HTTP client builder with optional upstream proxy.
     2→//!
     3→//! All crates that need a `reqwest::Client` with proxy support should use
     4→//! [`build_http_client`] rather than building their own, to keep proxy
     5→//! handling consistent.
     6→//!
     7→//! The gateway calls [`set_upstream_proxy`] once at startup so that any
     8→//! crate can later retrieve the URL via [`upstream_proxy_url`] without
     9→//! needing it threaded through every constructor.
    10→
    11→static UPSTREAM_PROXY: std::sync::OnceLock<String> = std::sync::OnceLock::new();
    12→
    13→/// Store the user-configured upstream proxy URL (call once at startup).
    14→pub fn set_upstream_proxy(url: &str) {
    15→    let _ = UPSTREAM_PROXY.set(url.to_string());
    16→}
    17→
    18→/// Return the upstream proxy URL, if one was configured.
    19→pub fn upstream_proxy_url() -> Option<&'static str> {
    20→    UPSTREAM_PROXY.get().map(String::as_str)
    21→}
    22→
    23→/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] with optional upstream proxy.
    24→///
    25→/// When `proxy_url` is `Some`, all requests are routed through that proxy
    26→/// (HTTP CONNECT for HTTPS targets, forward-proxy for plain HTTP).
    27→/// Localhost/loopback addresses are automatically excluded via `no_proxy`.
    28→///
    29→/// Supports `http://`, `https://`, `socks5://`, and `socks5h://` schemes.
    30→pub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {
    31→    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();
    32→    if let Some(url) = proxy_url
    33→        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)
    34→    {
    35→        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string("localhost,127.0.0.1,::1"));
    36→        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);
    37→    }
    38→    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())
    39→}
    40→
    41→/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] using the globally configured upstream proxy.
    42→///
    43→/// Convenience wrapper around [`build_http_client`] + [`upstream_proxy_url`].
    44→pub fn build_default_http_client() -> reqwest::Client {
    45→    build_http_client(upstream_proxy_url())
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Apply the upstream proxy to an existing [`reqwest::ClientBuilder`].
    49→///
    50→/// Useful when callers need additional builder options (timeout, redirect
    51→/// policy, etc.) but still want the global proxy applied.
    52→pub fn apply_proxy(mut builder: reqwest::ClientBuilder) -> reqwest::ClientBuilder {
    53→    if let Some(url) = upstream_proxy_url()
    54→        && let Ok(proxy) = reqwest::Proxy::all(url)
    55→    {
    56→        let proxy = proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string("localhost,127.0.0.1,::1"));
    57→        builder = builder.proxy(proxy);
    58→    }
    59→    builder
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Redact credentials from a proxy URL for safe logging.
    63→///
    64→/// Returns a version of the URL with `user:pass@` replaced by `***@`.
    65→/// If the URL cannot be parsed, returns `"<invalid proxy URL>"`.
    66→pub fn redact_proxy_url(url: &str) -> String {
    67→    // Parse minimally: look for `://user:pass@` pattern.
    68→    if let Some(scheme_end) = url.find("://") {
    69→        let after_scheme = &url[scheme_end + 3..];
    70→        if let Some(at_pos) = after_scheme.find('@') {
    71→            // Has userinfo — redact it.
    72→            let host_part = &after_scheme[at_pos..]; // includes '@'
    73→            return format!("{}://***{host_part}", &url[..scheme_end]);
    74→        }
    75→    }
    76→    // No credentials or unparseable — return as-is (no secrets to leak).
    77→    url.to_string()
    78→}
    79→
    80→#[cfg(test)]
    81→mod tests {
    82→    use super::*;
    83→
    84→    #[test]
    85→    fn no_proxy_returns_default_client() {
    86→        let client = build_http_client(None);
    87→        // Smoke test: the client was created without error.
    88→        drop(client);
    89→    }
    90→
    91→    #[test]
    92→    fn valid_http_proxy() {
    93→        let client = build_http_client(Some("http://127.0.0.1:8080"));
    94→        drop(client);
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    #[test]
    98→    fn valid_socks5_proxy() {
    99→        let client = build_http_client(Some("socks5://127.0.0.1:1080"));
   100→        drop(client);
   101→    }
   102→
   103→    #[test]
   104→    fn invalid_proxy_url_falls_back() {
   105→        // Garbage URL: should fall back to a plain client, not panic.
   106→        let client = build_http_client(Some("not-a-url"));
   107→        drop(client);
   108→    }
   109→
   110→    #[test]
   111→    fn apply_proxy_with_none_is_passthrough() {
   112→        // When no upstream proxy is set, apply_proxy is a no-op.
   113→        let builder = reqwest::Client::builder().timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(10));
   114→        let builder = apply_proxy(builder);
   115→        let client = builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new());
   116→        drop(client);
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    #[test]
   120→    fn redact_proxy_url_with_credentials() {
   121→        assert_eq!(
   122→            redact_proxy_url("http://user:p%40ss@proxy.example.com:8080"),
   123→            "http://***@proxy.example.com:8080"
   124→        );
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    #[test]
   128→    fn redact_proxy_url_without_credentials() {
   129→        assert_eq!(
   130→            redact_proxy_url("http://proxy.example.com:8080"),
   131→            "http://proxy.example.com:8080"
   132→        );
   133→    }
   134→
   135→    #[test]
   136→    fn redact_proxy_url_socks5_with_credentials() {
   137→        assert_eq!(
   138→            redact_proxy_url("socks5://admin:secret@10.0.0.1:1080"),
   139→            "socks5://***@10.0.0.1:1080"
   140→        );
   141→    }
   142→
   143→    #[test]
   144→    fn redact_proxy_url_user_only() {
   145→        // user without password still has '@'
   146→        assert_eq!(
   147→            redact_proxy_url("http://user@proxy:8080"),
   148→            "http://***@proxy:8080"
   149→        );
   150→    }

  "new_string": "/// Build a [`reqwest::Client`] with optional upstream proxy.\n///\n/// When `proxy_url` is `Some`, all requests are routed through that proxy\n/// (HTTP CONNECT for HTTPS targets, forward-proxy for plain HTTP).\n/// Localhost/loopback addresses are automatically excluded via `no_proxy`.\n///\n/// Supports `http://`, `https://`, `socks5://`, and `socks5h://` schemes.\npub fn build_http_client(proxy_url: Option<&str>) -> reqwest::Client {\n    let mut builder = reqwest::Client::builder();\n    if let Some(url) = proxy_url {\n        match reqwest::Proxy::all(url) {\n            Ok(proxy) => {\n                let proxy =\n                    proxy.no_proxy(reqwest::NoProxy::from_string(\"localhost,127.0.0.1,::1\"));\n                builder = builder.proxy(proxy);\n            },\n            Err(e) => {\n                tracing::warn!(\n                    error = %e,\n                    \"failed to parse upstream proxy URL, falling back to direct connection\"\n                );\n            },\n        }\n    }\n    builder.build().unwrap_or_else(|_| reqwest::Client::new())\n}"

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Uk1w7p4xSmLWa5YjWK8dyn
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/fir-author/crates/common/src/http_client.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Redact credentials from a proxy URL for safe logging.\n///\n/// Returns a version of the URL with `user:pass@` replaced by `***@`.\n/// If the URL cannot be parsed, returns `\"<invalid proxy URL>\"`.\npub fn redact_proxy_url(url: &str) -> String {\n    // Parse minimally: look for `://user:pass@` pattern.\n    if let Some(scheme_end) = url.find(\"://\") {\n        let after_scheme = &url[scheme_end + 3..];\n        if let Some(at_pos) = after_scheme.find('@') {\n            // Has userinfo — redact it.\n            let host_part = &after_scheme[at_pos..]; // includes '@'\n            return format!(\"{}://***{host_part}\", &url[..scheme_end]);\n        }\n    }\n    // No credentials or unparseable — return as-is (no secrets to leak).\n    url.to_string()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Redact credentials from a proxy URL for safe logging.\n///\n/// Returns a version of the URL with `user:pass@` replaced by `***@`.\n/// If the URL cannot be parsed, returns the URL as-is (no secrets to leak).\npub fn redact_proxy_url(url: &str) -> String {\n    // Parse minimally: look for `://user:pass@` pattern.\n    // Use `rfind('@')` so that literal `@` characters inside a\n    // percent-encoded password (e.g. `user:p%40ss@host`) are still\n    // fully redacted — the last `@` is always the userinfo delimiter.\n    if let Some(scheme_end) = url.find(\"://\") {\n        let after_scheme = &url[scheme_end + 3..];\n        if let Some(at_pos) = after_scheme.rfind('@') {\n            // Has userinfo — redact it.\n            let host_part = &after_scheme[at_pos..]; // includes '@'\n            return format!(\"{}://***{host_part}\", &url[..scheme_end]);\n        }\n    }\n    // No credentials or unparseable — return as-is (no secrets to leak).\n    url.to_string()\n}"
}
```

    30→```
    31→
    32→### What is proxied
    33→
    34→When `upstream_proxy` is set, the following traffic routes through the proxy:
    35→
    36→- **LLM provider API calls** (Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, etc.)
    37→- **Tool HTTP requests** (web fetch, web search, Firecrawl)
    38→- **OAuth flows** (device auth, token exchange)
    39→- **MCP server auth** (OAuth for remote MCP servers)
    40→- **Channel outbound** (Slack API calls, MS Teams API calls)
    41→
    42→Localhost and loopback addresses (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`) are
    43→automatically excluded from the proxy (`no_proxy`).
    44→
    45→### Telegram caveat
    46→
    47→Telegram uses [teloxide](https://github.com/teloxide/teloxide) which bundles
    48→its own HTTP client (reqwest 0.11). The `upstream_proxy` config does not apply
    49→to Telegram traffic directly. To proxy Telegram, set the standard
    50→`HTTPS_PROXY` environment variable, which teloxide's reqwest honours:
    51→
    52→```bash
    53→export HTTPS_PROXY=http://proxy.corp.example.com:8080
    54→moltis
    55→```
    56→
    57→Or use the `env` section in `moltis.toml`:
    58→
    59→```toml
```

> AGENT

Pushed. All three P2 items addressed:

1. **`rfind('@')`** — redaction now splits on the last `@`, handling literal `@` in passwords correctly
2. **`warn!` on parse failure** — `build_http_client` now logs a warning when the proxy URL can't be parsed instead of silently falling back
3. **Slack `chat.postMessage` gap** — documented in `upstream-proxy.md` that `slack-morphism`'s hyper connector bypasses the proxy, with `HTTPS_PROXY` env var as workaround
